     # CLAUDE.md
     This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
     ## Project Overview
     GEMINI Code Analysis and PRD Generator - A Python-based pipeline that analyzes Java/JSP/GWT/JavaScript codebases, extracts structured information, indexes artifacts in Weaviate vector database, and generates
     Product Requirements Documents (PRDs) using AI (Ollama/GEMINI).
     The project integrates with GitHub Spec Kit for spec-driven development workflows.
     … +240 lines (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ I've created a comprehensive CLAUDE.md file for this repository. The file includes:

  What I Included:

  1. Project Overview - Clear description of the GEMINI code analysis pipeline and its integration with Spec Kit
  2. Architecture - Documented the 5-core pipeline stages (discover, extract, index, search, prd) and the artifact type system
  3. Common Commands organized by category:
    - Environment setup (venv, .env, services)
    - Pipeline execution (both convenience script and direct Python)
    - Weaviate management (Docker operations)
    - Testing (pytest commands)
    - Spec Kit integration (slash commands)
  4. Development Notes:
    - Configuration hierarchy and critical environment variables
    - OS-specific behavior (macOS vs Ubuntu)
    - Project structure with directory explanations
    - Key scripts and their purposes
    - Multi-project support capabilities
  5. Integration with Spec Kit - How to use generated PRDs in the Spec Kit workflow
  6. Troubleshooting - Common issues and diagnostic commands

  Key Insights from Analysis:

  - The project is a sophisticated pipeline connecting Java codebase analysis → Weaviate vector DB → LLM-based PRD generation → Spec Kit integration
  - It's OS-aware with different Docker Compose files for macOS/Ubuntu
  - Uses Ollama for local LLM inference and Weaviate for semantic search
  - The weaviate_stats.py utility is particularly useful for diagnostics
  - Spec Kit integration is fully set up with slash commands in .claude/ directory
  - The src directory appears to be empty, which I noted in the troubleshooting section

  The CLAUDE.md focuses on practical information that would help future Claude instances understand the architecture and execute common tasks efficiently.

           + long-running analysis jobs.
        72 +  
        73 +  ### IV. Performance Requirements
        74 +  
        75 +  The pipeline MUST handle large-scale Java codebases efficiently:
        76 +  
        77 +  - **Discovery Performance**: File discovery MUST process at >1000 files/second. Large codebases (>100k files) MUST stream results rather than loading all paths into memory.
        78 +  - **Extraction Performance**: Extraction MUST process at >50 Java files/second for typical enterprise code. Extraction errors in individual files MUST NOT block processing of other files.
        79 +  - **Indexing Performance**: Weaviate indexing MUST batch operations (minimum 50 objects per batch). Indexing MUST be idempotent (re-running updates existing objects rather than duplicating).
        80 +  - **Search Performance**: Vector search queries MUST complete in <2 seconds for typical queries. Search MUST support project filtering without full collection scans.
        81 +  - **Memory Management**: Pipeline stages MUST process large codebases in streaming fashion. Peak memory usage MUST NOT exceed 2GB for processing 100k+ file codebases.
        82 +  - **Resource Cleanup**: All external connections (Weaviate, Ollama) MUST be properly closed after use. Failed operations MUST not leak resources.
        83 +  
        84 +  **Rationale**: Enterprise Java codebases can contain 100k+ files across multiple subprojects. Memory-efficient streaming and batching prevent OOM errors. Idempotent indexing enables incremental updates 
           + without reprocessing entire codebases.
        85 +

        87 +  
        88 +  All pipeline operations MUST be observable for debugging and optimization:
        89 +  
        90 +  - **Metrics Collection**: Each pipeline stage MUST log summary metrics (files discovered, artifacts extracted, objects indexed, search results). Metrics MUST be logged in structured format for parsing.
        91 +  - **Diagnostic Tools**: The weaviate_stats.py tool MUST be maintained to diagnose indexing issues. All diagnostic tools MUST output human-readable summaries with actionable insights.
        92 +  - **Progress Tracking**: Long-running operations MUST emit progress updates every 5 seconds or 1000 items processed (whichever is sooner). Progress MUST include estimated completion time when possible.
        93 +  - **Error Aggregation**: Pipeline MUST collect and summarize errors by type (e.g., "150 JSP parse errors, 23 missing dependencies"). Detailed errors MUST be logged at DEBUG level.
        94 +  - **Integration Health**: Pipeline MUST validate external service health (Ollama accessible, Weaviate schema exists) before starting operations. Health checks MUST provide clear status and remediation 
           + steps.
        95 +  
        96 +  **Rationale**: Analyzing large codebases can take hours. Detailed metrics and progress tracking enable users to identify bottlenecks, estimate completion times, and debug issues without rerunning entire
           +  pipelines.

           + and scope of the codebase before deeper analysis.

        21

        23 +

        25 +

           + scanning.

           + identifies key entities (classes, methods, interfaces), and tags it with relevant categories (layer, domain, frameworks, concerns).
        41

        43 +

        45 +

        47 +

        49 +

       168 +  
       169 +  - **Project**: Represents a Maven project root, containing Maven coordinates (groupId, artifactId, version), packaging type, module list, dependency information, framework usage, source root

           + chunked), content hash, and vector embedding for semantic search.
       172 +

           + statistics about discovered content.
       174 +  
       175 +  - **ExtractionResult**: Intermediate structure containing AI-generated understanding of a file, including classification refinement, natural language summary, identified entities, generated tags, 
           + detected frameworks and technologies, confidence scores, and any parsing errors or warnings.

⏺ Feature Specification Complete! ✅

  Summary

  I've successfully created a comprehensive feature specification for the Java Codebase Indexer Pipeline based on the requirements in IT20_1.md.

  Branch & Files Created

  - Branch: 001-java-codebase-indexer
  - Spec File: specs/001-java-codebase-indexer/spec.md
  - Checklist: specs/001-java-codebase-indexer/checklists/requirements.md

  Specification Overview

  The specification defines a Python CLI pipeline with four prioritized user stories:

  1. P1 - Discover and Catalog Java Projects (MVP): Scan directory trees, identify Maven projects, classify files, and create structured inventory
  2. P2 - Extract Semantic Understanding with AI: Use local Ollama to generate summaries, identify entities, and tag files with semantic metadata
  3. P3 - Index for Semantic Search: Store artifacts in Weaviate with vector embeddings for semantic search capabilities
  4. P4 - Monitor and Validate Status: Provide observability into indexed content with status commands

  Question 1 of 5: Indexed Data Lifecycle

  Context: The spec defines idempotent indexing (FR-012) and a reset option (FR-013), but doesn't specify how long indexed data should persist or when it should be purged.

  What we need to know: What is the intended lifecycle for indexed project data in Weaviate?

  Recommended: Option A - Industry-standard practice for code analysis tools is to keep indexed data indefinitely until explicitly reset or the project is re-indexed, which avoids data loss and supports historical queries. This aligns
  with your idempotent indexing design.

  | Option | Description                                                                                                              |
  |--------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
  | A      | Persist indefinitely - Data remains until user explicitly resets or re-indexes a project (aligns with idempotent design) |
  | B      | Time-based expiration - Auto-purge data older than N days/months (requires timestamp tracking)                           |
  | C      | Size-based limits - Purge oldest projects when storage exceeds threshold (requires storage monitoring)                   |
  | D      | Manual-only cleanup - No automatic purging, user must use reset command for each project (simplest implementation)       |

  You can reply with the option letter (e.g., "A"), accept the recommendation by saying "yes" or "recommended", or provide your own short answer.

> A

  Question 2 of 5: Concurrent Operation Safety

  Context: The spec describes discovery, extraction, and indexing as separate pipeline stages, but doesn't specify whether multiple operations can run simultaneously on different projects or the same project.

  What we need to know: Can multiple pipeline operations run concurrently?

  Recommended: Option B - Allow concurrent operations on different projects but prevent concurrent operations on the same project. This provides good throughput for teams analyzing multiple codebases while preventing data corruption
  from race conditions. Most code analysis tools follow this pattern.

  | Option | Description                                                                                                                  |
  |--------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
  | A      | Fully concurrent - Multiple operations can run simultaneously on any projects (requires locking mechanisms and coordination) |
  | B      | Per-project locking - Operations on different projects can run concurrently, but same project is locked (balanced approach)  |
  | C      | Single operation only - Only one pipeline operation can run at a time system-wide (simplest, but limits throughput)          |
  | D      | Stage-specific - Discovery and status can run concurrently, but extraction and indexing are exclusive (complex rules)        |

  You can reply with the option letter (e.g., "B"), accept the recommendation by saying "yes" or "recommended", or provide your own short answer.

> B

  ---
  Question 3 of 5: Multiple Project Version Handling

  Context: Maven projects have version numbers, and developers often maintain multiple versions of the same project (e.g., v1.0, v2.0, development branch). The spec mentions using Maven coordinates including version for project IDs
  (FR-011).

  What we need to know: How should the system handle the same project with multiple versions?

  Recommended: Option A - Treat each version as a separate project with distinct project IDs. This is the most flexible approach - users can index multiple versions for comparison, migration planning, or historical analysis. Each
  version maintains independent data in Weaviate.

  | Option | Description                                                                                                                       |
  |--------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
  | A      | Separate projects - Each version gets unique project ID (groupId:artifactId:version), all versions can coexist in Weaviate        |
  | B      | Latest only - Only the highest version number is indexed, older versions are automatically replaced during indexing               |
  | C      | Explicit version selection - User must specify which version to index via CLI flag, system rejects multiple versions without flag |
  | D      | Version grouping - All versions share base project ID, searchable as a group but distinguishable by version metadata              |

  You can reply with the option letter (e.g., "A"), accept the recommendation by saying "yes" or "recommended", or provide your own short answer.

> A

  Question 4 of 5: AI Service Rate Limiting

  Context: The system makes AI calls to Ollama for every file during extraction (FR-005, FR-006, FR-007). With large codebases processing thousands of files, this could potentially overwhelm the local Ollama service.

  What we need to know: Should the system implement rate limiting for AI calls to Ollama?

  Recommended: Option B - Implement configurable rate limiting with sensible defaults (e.g., max 10 concurrent requests). This prevents overwhelming the local Ollama service while maintaining good throughput. Most production systems use
   this pattern for external service integration.

  | Option | Description                                                                                                                               |
  |--------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
  | A      | No rate limiting - Send AI requests as fast as possible, rely on Ollama's internal queuing (simplest, but risks service degradation)      |
  | B      | Configurable rate limit - Max concurrent requests configurable via environment (e.g., MAX_CONCURRENT_AI_CALLS=10), with sensible defaults |
  | C      | Adaptive throttling - Monitor Ollama response times and automatically slow down if service becomes slow (complex, requires monitoring)    |
  | D      | Fixed delay - Insert fixed delay between each AI call (simple but inefficient, wastes time)                                               |

  You can reply with the option letter (e.g., "B"), accept the recommendation by saying "yes" or "recommended", or provide your own short answer.

> B

  Question 5 of 5: Empty State User Experience

  Context: The spec defines happy path scenarios but doesn't explicitly address what users see when discovery finds no projects, extraction finds no processable files, or status shows no indexed data.

  What we need to know: How should the system present empty state scenarios to users?

  Recommended: Option A - Informative messages with next steps. This is industry best practice for CLI tools - clear feedback about what happened and actionable guidance helps users understand whether they have a configuration issue or
  simply haven't indexed anything yet.

  | Option | Description                                                                                                                                                      |
  |--------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
  | A      | Informative messages - Display clear message explaining the empty state with suggested next steps (e.g., "No Maven projects found. Check JAVA_SOURCE_DIR path.") |
  | B      | Silent success - Return success exit code with minimal output, user must infer empty state from absence of results (minimal but unclear)                         |
  | C      | Warning status - Treat empty results as warnings with exit code 1, forcing users to acknowledge (strict but may be overly aggressive)                            |
  | D      | Interactive prompts - Ask user if they want to retry with different paths or configurations (helpful but breaks automation)                                      |

  You can reply with the option letter (e.g., "A"), accept the recommendation by saying "yes" or "recommended", or provide your own short answer.

> A

  Key Design Decisions

  1. Architecture: Single CLI application with streaming pipeline
  2. Technology Stack: Python 3.8+, Click, httpx, lxml, weaviate-client, pytest
  3. Concurrency: Threading with Semaphore (10 concurrent AI requests default)
  4. Storage: Weaviate for persistence, JSONL for intermediate data
  5. Performance: Streaming generators, batched writes, deterministic UUIDs
  6. Safety: Per-project file locking, idempotent indexing, retry logic

  Project Structure

  src/codeindex/
  ├── cli/         # 5 commands (discover, extract, index, search, status)
  ├── models/      # 4 entities
  ├── services/    # 8 services (discovery, maven, classifier, extractor, etc.)
  ├── parsers/     # 4 parsers (java, jsp, xml, sql)

  🚀 How to Use

  First Time Setup:
  # 1. Run setup script
  ./step1.sh

  # 2. Configure environment
  cp .env.example .env
  # Edit .env with your settings

  # 3. Start Weaviate
  ./docker-weaviate.sh start

  # 4. Test CLI
  ./with-venv.sh codeindex --help

  Daily Usage:

  # Option 1: Use wrapper script
  ./with-venv.sh codeindex discover --source-dir /path/to/java
  ./with-venv.sh pytest tests/unit/

  # Option 2: Activate manually
  source .venv/bin/activate
  codeindex --help
  pytest
  deactivate

  # Option 3: Run full pipeline
  ./run.sh [project-name]

       101            if stem.endswith(suffix):

       106 +      if stem.startswith('Test'):
       107 +          # Check if it ends with a test suffix (already checked above)
       108 +          # Or if it's a simple pattern like "TestSomething" (no Utils, Helper, Base, etc.)
       109 +          utility_keywords = ['Utils', 'Util', 'Helper', 'Support', 'Base', 'Abstract']
       110 +          is_utility = any(keyword in stem for keyword in utility_keywords)
       111 +          if not is_utility:
       112                return True
       113

⏺ Phase 5: US3 Index implementation is now complete! Here's what was accomplished:

  Phase 5 Summary - Weaviate Integration

  Completed Components:

  1. Weaviate Schema Definitions (src/codeindex/schemas/weaviate.py)
    - Project class schema (Maven project metadata)
    - CodeArtifact class schema (code files with AI semantics)
    - Schema management functions (create, validate, health check)
  2. Weaviate Store Service (src/codeindex/services/weaviate_store.py)
    - Client wrapper with connection management
    - Project indexing with update/create logic
    - Batch artifact indexing (configurable batch size)
    - Semantic search with filters (project, artifact type)
    - Statistics and health check operations
  3. Indexing Service (src/codeindex/services/indexing.py)
    - Orchestrates loading of discovery inventory and extraction results
    - Batch indexing with error recovery
    - Converts between file formats and Weaviate objects
  4. Index CLI Command (src/codeindex/cli/index.py)
    - codeindex index command with options for batch size, dry-run
    - Progress reporting and error handling
    - Integrates with discovery and extraction outputs
  5. Search CLI Command (src/codeindex/cli/search.py)
    - codeindex search command for semantic search
    - Filters by project and artifact type
    - Shows summaries, entities, frameworks, distance scores

  All imports verified and CLI help working correctly!

  Next: Phase 6 - US4 Status command implementation
